### Fixed
- **Integrate-failure reopen loop parks as `integration-pending` instead of reopening**: `failIntegration` no longer reopens a work-complete bead to the ready queue when integrate/push fails (srv-jobfile 2026-10-02: GitHub 403s re-queued beads whose work was done, session resume is disabled → ~11 full LLM spawns/45min re-doing finished work per bead). The bead now stays parked in place with a durable `integration-pending` label and the mechanical integration-retest stage owns the retry (it selects pending beads and strips the label on land). Every respawn path — scheduler retry queue, DAG ready filter, bead discovery, Charon orphan recovery, orphan-claim reconcile, and the health-digest OrphanClaims counter — skips/ignores the label. At `maxConsecutiveIntegrationFailures` the existing `integration-exhausted` hard-park still applies (Pan `clear-hard-park` remains the exit).
- **Pan wedge-bead cap bypass**: `filePanBead` counted only *open* pan-filed beads against `MaxOpenFiledBeadsPerProject`, so any filed bead claimed by a worker (goes to in_progress) freed its slot and subsequent filings bypassed the cap; same-title dedupe had the same hole. The cap and dedupe now count all non-terminal (open + in_progress) pan-filed beads; only closed/completed frees a slot (tests `TestFilePanBead_CapCountsInProgress`, `TestFilePanBead_DuplicateInProgressTitleSkipped`).
- **Gate deaths with no escalation** (ee5r/lkbo/ejsk 2026-10-04: three beads died at daemon gates in one 30-minute window and nobody was alerted): the verified-close escalation ladder is now durable — every rejection writes a `verified-close-reject-attempt-N` label and `validationRetryCount` takes the max of the in-memory count and the durable label, so a daemon restart or Charon orphan-reopening can no longer reset the ladder to zero and let gate failures cycle forever without reaching max rounds. The fleet health digest also alerts on silent gate deaths: `gatefailed` counts non-terminal beads carrying `gate-failed` without `escalated`/`needs-human`, `closedfail` counts beads closed-failed within 4h, both fire P0 campaign anomalies so a Pan judgment wakes on the death itself.
- **Scheduler must not execute pan-filed beads** (hydra-bldo): `filterByDAG` — the chokepoint all discovery paths return through — now drops pan-filed candidates (even with no DAG loaded), and the spawn funnel blocks pan-filed-labeled beads with a typed `spawn_blocked_human_triage` suppression. `spawnWorker` refreshes store label truth alongside Type/Project so a triage label Pan added while a stale copy was queued is honored. Pan file-bead filings are human triage, never worker-executable work.
- **Pan wedge-bead filing verifies live state first (jm2 guard)** (`internal/core/pan_health.go`, `internal/pan/pan.go`): `filePanBead` now re-probes before creating a wedge bead — refuses when the digest backing the judgment is older than 15m (`pan_bead_filed_skipped` event, reason `digest_stale`), when the judgment source bead has gone terminal (`source_resolved`), or when a live registry worker is mid-flight on it (`worker_live`). 2026-10-05: srv-jobfile-jm2 was filed claiming "no worker, no worktree" while the worktree existed and the worker had been live minutes earlier — the digest snapshot was stale by filing time. Probes fail open (probe error ≠ proof the premise is false), so genuine wedges stay visible.
- **PrefixGuard no longer renames mid-life stores**: when a store's existing `issue_prefix` equals the repo basename, the guard now distinguishes br's true default (written as a commented `# issue_prefix:` line, or no config at all) from an explicitly configured prefix by reading `.beads/config.yaml` directly; an explicit value is preserved with a loud log, and an unreadable store config (WAL-poisoned `br config get` flap) preserves the established namespace rather than gambling a rename. Root cause of the 2026-10-05 srv-jobfile split namespace: 38 `srv-jobfile-*` beads vs 11 post-rename `ajantha-srv-jobfile-*` beads — every Pan-filed wedge bead and fix bead after 10-05 04:01 carried the renamed prefix while deps/fix-original references kept the old one (`[DAG] Dependency not found: srv-jobfile-4eg -> srv-jobfile-65l`).
- **Epic auto-close over open children when compile-time parent-child links failed** (srv-jobfile master epic closed with 33 open children): when a child never got its epic dependency link (br single-parent constraint), neither the DAG nor the Dependencies-based close guard could see the open children, and the last defense — br's own "parent issue has N open children" close skip — was being pushed through with `--force`. `closeBeadOnce` now verifies bead type on a skip and refuses to force-close epics (fail-closed on unverifiable type; terminal epics still pass); the overseer classifies br's open-children refusal as an `epic_close_refused` (reason `open_children`) event and throttles re-checks. Operators: an epic that stays open with everything "closed" in the DAG means br still sees open children — check `br show <epic>` children, then `br dep add` the missing links.

- Digest no longer counts an in_progress bead with a `state=completed` registry row as an orphaned claim — a completed row is terminal worker state and the scheduler's respawn backoff owns the requeue; counting it re-fired the P0 campaign-anomaly filing loop every sweep (hydra 2026-10-06 13:4x: 15 beads between worker attempts, 5 pan-filed twins in 12 min). Genuine orphans (no row past the claim grace, or a working/hung row with stale heartbeat) still count. Regression test `TestCollectHealthDigestCompletedRowNotOrphan`; triage at `docs/discovery/orphanclaim-completed-row-triage-xcye.md`.

- Post-close sweep GREEN: internal/core/postclose_sweep.go scan() now re-verifies recently closed beads (metadata test_command, ≤24h) with a one-retry flake guard (pass → `postclose-flake`), reopen with both failing outputs (`postclose-reopened`), and bounded second-failure escalation (`needs-human`); real hourly Start loop. Fixes the 4 failing pinned RED tests.

- Post-close sweep GREEN (hydra-96th): implemented `PostCloseSweeper.scan()` — reopen path (comment with BOTH failing-run outputs, StatusOpen, `postclose-reopened` label, `postclose_reopened` event), flake guard (`postclose-flake`, no reopen), bounded second-failure (`needs-human`, no second reopen), 24h/commandless skips, hourly Start/Stop loop, nil-safe + fail-open. Unblocks the RED suite left failing by the inert scaffold (4 gate failures).

- Orphan-claim anomaly disposition (hydra-v5xq): the persisting "N in_progress bead(s) with no live registry worker" digest anomaly was escalated verified-close beads (k991/kvsc/ee5r), not orphans — recovery and digest exclusion behaved correctly. Beads closed as landed after origin/development integration checks; triage at `docs/discovery/orphanclaim-escalation-loop-triage-v5xq.md`. Disk-99% claim stale (host 49%).
### Added

- Tool-health probe framework (GREEN, `internal/core/tool_health.go`): `ToolHealthSweeper` with four default probes — hindsight (GET `/health` 200 + "healthy"; POST `.../v1/default/banks/hydra/memories/recall` 200 + parseable JSON), cass (`cm context "tool-health probe" --json`, exit 0 + `success:true`), graphify (per project: `graphify-out/graph.json` exists and mtime < 2× `intelligence.graphify.refresh_interval`), llm-router (GET health URL → 200). Per-tool state latching: exactly one `tool_degraded` per degradation, one `tool_recovered` per recovery, zero events while healthy. Each probe bounded by `DefaultProbeTimeout` (10s). Wired via `shepherd.tool_health` config (enabled/interval_seconds/hindsight_base_url/llm_router_url/graphify_refresh_interval; disabled by default) with daemon Start/Stop in `service_starter.go`; config example + shepherd doc updated.
- RED: tool-health probe framework contract tests (`internal/core/tool_health_test.go`, 10 `TestToolHealthContract*` tests) pinning the shepherd tool-health sweep: per-tool latched `tool_degraded`/`tool_recovered` events, 10s per-probe timeout, `shepherd.tool_health.enabled: false` disables the sweep goroutine, and contract probes for hindsight (health + recall endpoints), cass (`cm context --json` success:true), graphify (per-project graph.json freshness vs 2× refresh interval), and llm-router health. Inert stub in `tool_health.go` keeps the package compiling; GREEN implements.
- Project-path existence guard for graphify refresh: `GraphifyIndexer.Refresh` stats the absolute project path before claiming a build — a nonexistent path returns `false` and spawns no build goroutine. First miss emits one typed `project_path_missing` event (with `path` field, rate-limited to once per 24h per path); a path that reappears clears its missing marker and refreshes normally. Event sink wired from the overseer.

### Fixed

- Triage: P0 orphan-claim + disk-99% anomaly (hydra-cgud) verified stale — registry showed 7 working rows with fresh heartbeats, Charon reopened the full orphan cohort (lz3m/cw35/bldo/95ia/nydy/lkbo/2a3f/s8l7), and `df -h /` = 49%; recovery and disk-cleanup guard already landed. `docs/discovery/orphanclaim-disk99-triage-cgud.md`.
- Orphaned-claims digest false positive: `collectHealthDigest` now excludes `in_progress` beads whose close gates have a live running artifact (same probe Charon uses, `GateCloseInProgress`), and gate artifacts record the gate child PID instead of the daemon's — a stale `running` marker carrying the daemon PID stayed "alive" forever, so Charon's `FinalizeStaleGateArtifacts` could never interrupt it and mid-close beads kept re-firing the P0 "orphaned claims" campaign anomaly every judgment round (hydra 2026-10-06: 6 beads whose workers had exited 0 minutes earlier). *Authored by a Hydra dogfood run.*

- Pan-filed beads excluded from orphan-claim handling on both sides: the digest's `OrphanClaims` counter and Charon orphan recovery no longer treat `pan-filed` triage/campaign beads as orphaned claims. These filings legitimately sit `in_progress` past the 5m grace (awaiting spawn or close gates), so the P0 campaign-anomaly digest re-fired on its own filings — 9 twin P0 filings in 25 min on 2026-10-06, plus Charon destructively reopening pan-filed triage beads (hydra-2a3f). The file-bead cap owns their lifecycle.
- Charon orphan recovery no longer respawns a duplicate worker while the bead's close is running (or queued behind) daemon gates: it now probes the artifact-first gate markers (`$HYDRA_HOME/gates/*/<bead>/*.json` with `status=running`, live writer PID, <45m old) before reopening an orphan `in_progress` bead. Root cause of the mo66 duplicate-spawn loop: a long sibling `test` gate holds the per-project gate lock past the 5m orphan grace, so the close looked like an orphan (kf2t 2026-09-27, mo66 2026-10-06).
- Credential preflight parks now recover on their own: a project whose origin probe passes again is auto-unparked (only when the park reason is the preflight's own `auto-parked: GitHub credential…`), with a `credential_recovered` event. A new 10-minute sweep re-probes credential-parked projects so recovery no longer waits for a daemon restart — hydra itself sat parked 26h past its PAT fix.
### Documentation
- Push-pending wedge triage (hydra-2a3f): the 4-BLOCKED-beads premise was stale — no blocked beads in any store and the hydra checkout fully published; the per-tick `publish_push_rejected` churn against standing-open PRs (root cause of the pseudo-bead accumulation) was fixed and deployed same-day via the idempotent `publish_pr_waiting` skip (PR #250). Residual: conflicted PRs #144 (fms-frontend) and #417 (srv-telematics) need human/Tier-B conflict resolution before merge. Resolved same bead: both PRs conflict-resolved via plumbing merge onto current bases and admin-merged (#144 MERGED 191c7ad 11:38:58Z; #417 MERGED 8d33c6e 11:40:13Z) — no push-pending remains on the host.

### Added
- RED tests for Evidence-of-use probes (deep tier) (internal/core/tool_health_evidence_test.go): pin `IntelUsageEvent` (hindsight/cass/graphify_fallback fields), the evidence-tier sweep emitting `tool_degraded` with `probe:"evidence"` when worker activity is recorded but `hindsight_memories` is zero, zero worker activity emitting nothing, and fail-open probe errors; pins the `ToolHealthEvidenceScanner` API GREEN must implement; internal/core/tool_health_evidence.go holds an inert RED stub (compile-clean, zero behavior — tests fail at runtime).
- **Adversarial close gate (bounded veto)** (`internal/core/adversary_gate.go`, `validation.agent_verifier`): after the deterministic close gates pass, a pi-backed reviewer scores the diff against the bead's acceptance criteria. Ships when zero BLOCKER findings and score ≥ `min_score` (default 0.8); NITs never block. Rejects ride the standard max_rounds reopen/escalate path with blocker feedback injected into the bead description; a different blocker set per round trips the churn guard and ships with dissent (`adversary_churn` event, `closed-with-findings` label). Verifier errors fail open with `adversary_error` events. Resurrects the dead AgentVerifier class (hardcoded localhost endpoint, silent skips, zero verdicts ever) as a Pan-shaped pi invocation with events for measurement.
### Added
- RED tests for the post-close verification sweep (internal/core/postclose_sweep_test.go): pin reopen-with-both-outputs, flake-guard label-without-reopen, bounded second-reopen → needs-human, skip of >24h/commandless beads, and a real-git integration reopen; pins `NewPostCloseSweeper` API GREEN must implement; internal/core/postclose_sweep.go holds an inert RED stub (compile-clean for the lint gate, zero behavior — tests fail at runtime with contract assertions).
- Post-close verification sweep GREEN (internal/core/postclose_sweep.go): hourly re-verification of beads closed within 24h carrying `metadata["test_command"]` — flake guard (fail→pass re-run → `postclose-flake` label, no reopen), single reopen with `postclose-reopened` label + comment carrying both failing-run outputs, repeat failure → `needs-human` instead of a second reopen; typed events, all fail-open. (Re-landed as b5cf4f9e after integration merges had restored the RED stub and dropped the GREEN strand.)

### Documentation
- host-setup.md config-knob table now documents the `validation.agent_verifier` block (enabled/min_score/classes/max_diff_bytes/timeout_seconds/provider/model/pi_path) with a pointer to shepherd.md §1a.

### Fixed
- **Spawn-path worker-already-exists race**: `WorkerSpawner.isRegistryWorkerLive` now reconciles a non-terminal registry row whose heartbeat is stale (>5m, Charon's `heartbeatHungAfter`) AND whose PID is dead/unset as `orphan-reaped` (new `registry.MarkCompletedWithReason`) instead of failing every respawn with "worker already exists" until the hourly ghost reap — the spawn-failed cohort wedge (beads blocked >1m, 2026-10-06). Fresh-heartbeat rows stay blocking (PID-reuse / reporter.Stop-in-flight protection); alive-PID rows stay blocking (hang path belongs to Charon); reconciliation write errors fail closed.
- **Charon orphan-recovery suppression**: the registry liveness check in `beadHasLiveWorker` now requires state=working AND a live PID — a stale non-working row with a fresh-enough heartbeat suppressed orphan recovery for minutes (srv-jobfile-dh0).

### Changed
- Evidence sweep refactor: `ToolHealthEvidenceScanner.ready()` unifies the probe-nil guard shared by Start/scan, `emitDegraded` extracts the tool_degraded emission, the 24h activity window is a named package const, and the worker's `emitIntelUsageEvent` split into `intelUsageRecord` + `appendEventLine` — behavior unchanged.
- **Publish idempotence + honest waiting signal**: a tier-policy "PR left open awaiting human merge" no longer emits `publish_push_rejected` (reason `pr_merge_failed`) on every publisher tick. First wait emits `publish_pr_waiting` (reason `pr_awaiting_human`) with the PR URL; later ticks skip entirely while the branch tip is unchanged (process-local tip mark; new work on the branch re-publishes). Real failures (credential-class, conflicts, API) still emit `publish_push_rejected`. 2026-10-06: 114 rejected events/day across 3 repos were routine waiting reading as failure, and the misnomer fed an audit panic about unpublished work that did not exist.

### Added
- **Deploy md5 gate** (`scripts/verify-deploy.sh`): new optional 4th argument `build-dir` asserts the installed daemon/worker binaries are byte-identical (md5) to the just-built artifacts. Freshness and restart checks cannot catch a silent no-op install — on 2026-10-06 two deploy chains reported `active` while yesterday's binary stayed installed, costing a phantom-debugging cycle. The §10 deploy procedure now mandates the gate.
### Added
- `pan.escalated_leaf_grace_seconds` (default 86400): how long an escalated bead with no dependents sits before Pan sees it as a stale-escalation candidate. Pan's stale-escalation recipe also gained the leaf-grace exception: a reason reporting "beyond the leaf grace" overrides the "~2 days: leave blocked" default, so the judge actually acts on early candidates instead of silently watching them. The leaf reason now prefers `repair:clear-escalation` (one fair retry; re-fires on merit), so escalations caused by a daemon defect fixed by a later deploy get auto-retried instead of idling overnight for a human (srv-jobfile-eci: gate-extraction defect fixed by deploy, bead idled escalated with no path back). Lower the knob to cycle defect-class verified-close escalations within the hour.
### Fixed
- Verification-command extractor truncated commands containing a backtick run (e.g. `grep -c '\`\`\`'` in a docs check): substring fence matching cut at the embedded ``` → `/bin/sh: Syntax error: Unterminated quoted string` → false verification-failed escalations (srv-jobfile-eci). Fence matching is now line-based.
- Close-time hard gates received an empty worktree path from the close caller and fell back to the main checkout (older package.json, no node_modules): verification commands failed with `Missing script` / exit 127. The caller now resolves the bead's chain worktree (shared helper with the daemon-gates block).
- Close-gate verification ran in the daemon's cwd, not the worktree: `gateExecutableAcceptance` computed `dir` but never set `cmd.Dir`, so every repo-relative verification command (e.g. `npm run test:e2e`) failed with `npm error code ENOENT` and verified-close rejected every such bead 3/3, escalating whole chains (srv-jobfile 2026-10-05: 8bj/djq/h7c/98k/fcn/xhe). The rejection detail even printed the dir it never executed in. Regression test: cwd-sensitive marker file.
- Wedged-escalation recovery loop (srv-jobfile 2026-10-05 stall): an escalated bead is in_progress with no worker by construction, so Charon orphan-recovery correctly refuses it and — if it heads a dependency chain — the whole campaign starves (2 escalated beads → br ready=0, zero spawns for 3.5h). `panBlockedReason` now surfaces an escalated bead to Pan after `pan.escalated_wedge_grace_seconds` (default 1h) when it still has live dependents, with a reason naming the wedge; the Pan rescue recipe prefers `repair:clear-escalation` for wedged chain heads while leaf escalations keep the 24h human window. Charon's escalated-skip now emits a cooldown-limited `orphan_escalated_wedged` typed event (was log-only, 378 duplicate lines in one morning). The wedge detector reads br's own `dependent_count` metadata (`br list --json` omits the dependencies list). Also revived the dead `EscalationTTLScanner`: its blanket `in_progress` skip could never fire because escalated beads are in_progress by construction — it now skips only beads with a live registry worker and scans multi-repo children via `repoScanTargets`.
- Wedged-escalation recovery loop (srv-jobfile 2026-10-05 stall): an escalated bead is in_progress with no worker by construction, so Charon orphan-recovery correctly refuses it and — if it heads a dependency chain — the whole campaign starves (2 escalated beads → br ready=0, zero spawns for 3.5h). `panBlockedReason` now surfaces an escalated bead to Pan after `pan.escalated_wedge_grace_seconds` (default 1h) when it still has live dependents, with a reason naming the wedge; the Pan rescue recipe prefers `repair:clear-escalation` for wedged chain heads while leaf escalations keep the 24h human window. Charon's escalated-skip now emits a cooldown-limited `orphan_escalated_wedged` typed event (was log-only, 378 duplicate lines in one morning). Also revived the dead `EscalationTTLScanner`: its blanket `in_progress` skip could never fire because escalated beads are in_progress by construction — it now skips only beads with a live registry worker and scans multi-repo children via `repoScanTargets`.
- Follow-up to hydra-k3nb L2: repos under a user's `type: multi` parent dir (e.g. `~/projects/srv-jobfile`) now attribute to that user in `getUserContextForProject` — previously only literal config paths matched, so every auto-discovered multi-repo child silently published under the daemon identity.
- Integration-branch namespace no longer leaks `dogfood/` into product repos (hydra-tvfd): per-bead PR branches, publish branches, and retest branches now use `dogfood/` only for daemon-owned repos (hydra self-improvement) and `hydra/` for user-owned product repos, via new `IntegrationBranchPrefix(projectPath)` wired from the overseer's daemon-owned predicate. PR triage accepts both prefixes. Existing `dogfood/*` branches on product remotes are untouched (open PRs reference them).
- Remote branch lifecycle (hydra-9dp5): auto-merged integration PRs now delete their source branch (`gh pr merge --delete-branch`), and each publish cycle sweeps remote `dogfood/`/`hydra/` branches whose PRs reached MERGED (covers human-merged tier-2 product PRs). Without this, one campaign left 88 permanent branches on srv-telematics.
- Per-user publish credentials (hydra-k3nb): (L1) the legacy non-PR publish push now embeds the resolved project-owner token (`PushBranchWithTokenNonFFRecovery`) instead of falling back to the ambient git credential helper, which silently authenticated product-repo pushes as the daemon owner. (L2) project→owner attribution now normalizes paths (Clean + EvalSymlinks) instead of exact-string matching — a formatting miss silently demoted user-owned projects to the daemon identity; misses on `/home/` paths now log a WARN.
- Pan-filed wedge beads deduped by error signature: `normalizeReason` now also strips filesystem paths, bead-ID tokens, and cardinal numbers (count variants like "17 vs 23 beads blocked" collapse to one signature), and the filer remembers filed signatures for 1h (`panFileSigTTL`) so a closed wedge bead is not re-filed while the same root cause still produces blocked beads — N blocked beads with one root cause now file exactly ONE wedge bead (was 15–20 per campaign, each spawning its own diagnostic worker). Filer errors still leave the window open for retry.
- `task-breakdown` shape routing wired end-to-end: `classifyRequirement` now applies the §0 `System Type` fallback (routing PRD rule (d)) — inside `cli-tool`/`analysis`/`docs` campaigns, requirements whose targets all live in the docs tree (rubric + verifier scripts included) classify as `direct_task` instead of spawning full TDD chains. The fms per-developer-review PRD now compiles to 6 direct_task + 2 verification_gate + 1 aggregation_task with zero chains. Pinned by 4 new assertions in `test-shape-classification.mjs`.
- Phantom `NFR-003 Monorepo Configuration` bead no longer injected for analysis/docs-shaped PRDs: the runner greenfield guard now fires only when at least one requirement classified `tdd_chain` (docs-only campaigns have no test harness to scaffold).
- `task-breakdown` parser: `**Depends On:**` sections are now actually parsed (list form and inline form) into `explicitDependsOn` — previously the header was recognized but its IDs were silently dropped, so declared chains never formed and same-file writers could schedule in parallel.
- `task-breakdown` parser: `**Reads From:**` entries no longer merge into `files` — they are read-only dependencies tracked in `readsFrom`, ending false `validateFileOverlap` failures when a docs FR reads another FR's file.
- `create-prd` SKILL: parser notes added (Depends On both forms, Reads From read-only, placeholder glyphs in Target Files parse as filenames).
- `task-breakdown` parser: `**Test Command:**` / `**Build Command:**` accept the fenced-block form the contract shows; multi-line blocks join with `&&`.
- `task-breakdown` runner: dep-ID mapping is pattern-aware (`terminalDepLocalId` — direct/config terminal bead is `-apply`, not hardcoded `-syntax`); requirement emission order is dependency-aware (Kahn pass over explicit Depends On — the priority sort placed a P0 tdd_chain before the P0 direct_task it depends on, crashing with `TDD dependency implement-<id>-apply not found`); discovery beads are skipped per requirement when Target Files are resolved or the pattern has no implementation surface (kills the discovery-inflation class — 44 junk beads in one srv-telematics campaign); direct_task beads get deliverable instructions instead of config-fusion wording ("do not write tests" was wrong for test-authoring direct tasks).

### Changed
- Refactored `BeadCloser.verifyBeforeClose` (internal/core/bead_closer.go) from a 207-line monolith into 9 focused helpers (`skipIfTerminal`, `verifyTDDChain`, `resolveEvidenceRepo`, `closeIntegrationBase`, `collectCloseEvidence`, `unionMainCheckoutLeaks`, `acceptAlreadyIntegrated`, `chainBranchIntegrated`, `worktreeHEADIntegratedLocally`) with the already-integrated heuristics' incident context preserved as doc comments; the integration base is now resolved once per close instead of twice (drops one duplicate mismatch log line). Behavior-preserving; full internal/core suite green (ok 366.6s).
- Refactored `internal/core/stranded_work.go` — duplicated bead-label writing, event-emission guards, and branch-key/bead-lookup/resumability logic extracted into named helpers (`worktreeKeyFromBranch`, `beadStateFor`, `beadIsResumable`, `hasAnyLabel`, `emitStrandedEvent`, `labelBead`). Behavior unchanged; all 14 stranded-work detector/repair tests pass.
- Refactored `internal/core/verification_runner.go` — result classification (`classifyResult`), run-error translation (`applyRunError`), provenance finalization (`finalizeProvenance`), event mapping (`verificationEvent`), and credential-env scrubbing (`appendUncredentialed`) extracted into named helpers with doc comments; unused provenance parameter removed from `execute`; behavior unchanged, all 8 verification-runner tests pass.
- Refactored `.agents/skills/task-breakdown/runner.mjs` Verification Command propagation — the three call sites that manually paired `buildVerificationCommand` + `verificationCommandBlock` (TDD phase loop, `createDirectTask`, configuration apply bead) now go through the single `applyVerificationContract` helper. Behavior byte-for-byte unchanged; all 6 task-breakdown suites exit 0.
- Refactored `internal/core/bead_closer_hard_gates.go` — three duplicated `events.EmitRepo` blocks unified into a single `emitGateEvent` helper, `close_dep_open` promoted to a named constant, dependency-order gate restructured singleton-first for readability. Behavior unchanged; all hard-gate + close-path tests pass.
- REFACTOR (hydra-hz50): decomposed the sandboxed verification runner (internal/core/verification_runner.go) into named single-responsibility helpers — `finalizeProvenance`, `classifyResult` (pass/vacuous rules), `applyRunError` (exit/timeout mapping), `verificationEvent` (typed event mapping) — with the unused provenance parameter removed from `execute`; behavior identical, 8/8 runner tests green.
- Integration sprint (human triage): merged the four implemented acceptance-postclose strands from their dogfood worktree branches to development — close-time hard gates FR-002 (b9xf), Verification Command contract (q98z), sandboxed verification runner (mmdx), stranded-work detector (5zcl). Full Go suite green (26 packages, 1308 tests in internal/core). RED-only postclose-sweep suite (ewe3) deliberately excluded until GREEN (rxi6) lands — failing tests do not ship. Stale beads uqrw/tf88/thtm closed as superseded-by-merge; 8 zombie auto-fix Fix: beads cancelled.
### Added
- RED-phase test suite `internal/core/bead_closer_hard_gates_test.go`: pins the three FR-002 close-time hard gates (executable acceptance with verbatim-output rejection, quarantine contradiction, dependency-order warn-only singletons) plus unchanged VerifiedClose round/escalation semantics. All 10 tests fail at RED with `runCloseHardGates undefined` — the GREEN bead implements the gate entry point.
- GREEN: `BeadCloser.runCloseHardGates` (internal/core/bead_closer_hard_gates.go) implements the three FR-002 close-time hard gates — executable acceptance (declared **Verification Command:** executed in the worktree, non-zero exit rejects with verbatim output via `close_gate_rejected`, missing command fails open with `verification_command_missing`), quarantine contradiction (`closed-vs-quarantined` while `hydra/quarantine/<id>` exists without an `integrated` label, git errors fail open), and dependency order (chain members hard-reject on non-terminal deps, singletons warn-only). Wired on the completed-close path before the VerifiedClose judge round; rejections reuse `rejectClose` so reopen/attempt-cap/escalation semantics are unchanged. ~90% statement coverage on the new code.
- task-breakdown GREEN: Verification Command contract implemented in `runner.mjs` — `buildVerificationCommand`/`verificationCommandBlock`/`applyVerificationContract` helpers; every tdd_chain/direct_task/configuration_task/aggregation_task bead carries `metadata.verification_command` (Test Command byte-for-byte, else Build Command + non-vacuity `&& echo` tail, else the tail alone) plus a matching `**Verification Command:**` fenced description block. configuration_task fuses its SYNTAX validation gate into the single APPLY bead (one bead per config requirement, validation steps + tdd-syntax/quality labels retained); `analyzer.mjs` exposes top-level `route` from `analyzePRDDeterministic` via a non-recursive `computeRoute` (routeTaskPattern↔analyze recursion bug). All 9 contract tests pass in default and VERIFICATION_COMMAND_ENFORCE=1 modes; 6 suites green.
- task-breakdown RED: `test-verification-command.mjs` pins the Verification Command contract — every tdd_chain/direct_task/configuration_task/aggregation_task bead carries `metadata.verification_command` + a `**Verification Command:**` fenced description block, equal to the requirement's Test Command byte-for-byte when present, else Build Command + non-vacuity wrapper. Suite exits 0 by default (feature absent → 9 loud skips); `VERIFICATION_COMMAND_ENFORCE=1` fails pre-GREEN (RED proof), matching the PRDCONTRACT_ENFORCE pattern.
- GREEN: sandboxed verification runner (internal/core/verification_runner.go) — argv-only exec (no shell layer), credential env scrubbing, process-group timeout kill with WaitDelay pipe-unblock, vacuous zero-output rejection, provenance capture, verification_passed/verification_failed events. 8/8 RED tests pass; new-code coverage ~85%.
- RED: behavior tests for the sandboxed verification runner (internal/core/verification_runner_test.go) — pin argv-only execution (injection string never interpreted by a shell), credential env scrubbing, timeout kill, vacuous zero-output rejection, provenance capture, and events on pass/fail paths. Fails against the missing implementation until the GREEN phase lands internal/core/verification_runner.go.
### Fixed
- stranded-work detector tests: added none-present-path test (clean repo → no units, no events) and pinned the `quarantine_branch` field of the `stranded_conflict` event payload (AC: event payload includes quarantine branch name when present); detector coverage 89.1% / repair 85.3%. Daily count of stranded-work events is queryable from the event stream via `jq -s '[.[] | select(.type=="worktree_stranded")] | length' <events-dir>/$(date -u +%Y-%m-%d).jsonl` (events live at `$HYDRA_HOME/events/YYYY-MM-DD.jsonl`).
- task-breakdown classifier: explicit `**Type:**` deliverable-shape markers (analysis/audit/test/refactor/spike/deletion) are now parsed into the requirement and override classification in `classifyRequirement` (→ `direct_task`), instead of being visible only to the standalone `routeTaskPattern` API while analysis PRDs compiled to TDD chains; shape-bearing header prefixes (TEST-/REF-/SPIKE-/AUDIT-/DEL-/ANALYSIS-) now also stop description accumulation like other requirement headers.
- feature: git-ownership onboarding preflight (fmsfe wedge) — at daemon start each project repo is probed with `git rev-parse` as the hydra user; an exit-128 "dubious ownership" refusal fails closed (`git_ownership_unusable` event + auto-park, detail carries the exact `git config --global --add safe.directory <path>` command; daemon never sets safe.directory itself). Spawn funnel gains the same check (`spawn_blocked_git_ownership`), and spawn-time occurrences map to the distinct `git-ownership` hard-park label + `spawn_failed_git_ownership` event so Pan's filing contains the fix command. Docs: host-setup.md git-ownership preflight section, shepherd.md event table. *Authored by a Hydra dogfood run.*

- Added: sensor symmetry — every detector now emits a symmetric fire+resolve typed-event pair under a shared `fields.correlation_id` (events.go `SensorCorrelation`): `verified_close_rejected`/`verified_close_passed`, `charon_orphan_reopened`+`orphan_recovered` (Charon orphanWatch resolve), `worktree_quarantined`/`quarantine_restored` (reconciler + retry-quarantine), `publish_rebase_attempted`+`publish_succeeded`, `aimd_scale_down`/`aimd_scale_up` (governor now has an event sink), `repair_failed`/`repair_succeeded` (all Pan repair verbs via wrapRepairHandlers). All emissions fail-open and never gate the pipeline; per-sensor fire/resolve rates computable via `scripts/sensor-rollup.sh` — the fine-tuning evidence previously missing (OrphanRetry 1,912 fires / 0 resolves; AIMD invisible).

- Disk-watermark cleanup guard (hydra-i6we P0 campaign-anomaly proposal, part 2): when the fleet digest detects disk usage at or above the watermark (85% on `/`), the daemon now runs a conservative `systemd-tmpfiles --clean` pass (30s timeout, once per hour cooldown, no `rm` ever) instead of only reporting the anomaly — the previous advice-only behavior let br degrade to I/O error 28 if no operator noticed. Fail-open: cleanup failures log only and never block the digest; typed `disk_cleanup_run` event on each pass. Tests: `TestDiskCleanupGuard_*` (6). *Authored by a Hydra dogfood run.*

- Fixed: Pan filed-bead cap is now a config knob (`shepherd.pan.max_filed_beads_per_project`, default 4, nil-safe fallback 2) instead of the hard-coded `pan.MaxOpenFiledBeadsPerProject = 2` — during the 2026-10-01 fms wedge storm the cap silently suppressed gap-bead filing (pan-filed=22 with 6+ wedge classes recurring) until wedges consumed the boards. A cap hit now emits a typed `pan_filed_cap_hit` event (fields `open`/`cap`) and adds a stable `pan-filed cap saturated` anomaly to that project in the Pan health digest (cleared on the next successful filing) so suppression is visible to Pan and operators. Tests: `TestFilePanBead_ConfigKnobCap`, `TestFilePanBead_DefaultCapFour`. *Authored by a Hydra dogfood run.*
- Daily token-usage rollup (fix-jwk8): token/turn spend trends were unmeasurable once the worker registry pruned rows (`ReapStale` drops completed rows after 24h; same-day completions were the only history queryable). Workers now persist their token totals into a durable `token_usage_daily` table (day+project primary key, additive accumulation: completions, tokens_fresh/output/cache, max per bead, rerun count, zero-token count) — on reap (`ReapStale`/`ReapGhosts` persist before delete) and via a daily job at 00:05 UTC that snapshots live rows and emits one typed `token_usage_daily` event per (day, project). Median per bead recomputed from the stored per-bead token list. Fail-open: registry/event errors are logged and retried next tick; never blocks the pipeline. Tests: `internal/registry/token_usage_daily_test.go`, `internal/core/token_usage_rollup_test.go`. *Authored by a Hydra dogfood run.*
- PR aging sweep (hydra-rrtq): closes that leave an integration PR open waiting on a human merge now record the PR in a durable ledger ($HYDRA_HOME/pr-waiting.json); a weekly scanner (overseer.pr_aging_days, default 7, 0 disables) probes each PR via gh, drops landed/closed ones, and emits one typed pr_aging_digest event per owner for PRs still open past the threshold — classified stuck-auto-merge (repo in pr_auto_merge_repos/deploy.repo_path, merge wedged) vs t2-human-merge-queue (designed Tier-2 stop state). Fail-open throughout. *Authored by a Hydra dogfood run.*
- create-prd producer contract documentation (hydra-cxpf, TDD validation round 1): SKILL.md §3.1a now documents the five-value System Type enum (`library`/`cli`/`api`/`application`/`ops`), the five chain prefixes (`RED:`/`GREEN:`/`REFACTOR:`/`SYNTAX:`/`FIX:`), and the strict classification precedence (prefix > target-file shape > System Type > tdd_chain); `tools/prd-lint` enforces valid System Type values (rule R9, first-token qualifier-tolerant) with contract + lint tests. *Authored by a Hydra dogfood run.*

### Fixed
- Orphan-claim/disk-99% re-triage (hydra-pf9c, duplicate of hydra-6d01): the 4 named in_progress beads (bxzr/1h4v/wiu6/cxpf) all carry `escalated` — human-gated tdd-validation-failed close rejections, not orphans; fix already landed (7548196). Charon reaper live-verified (4 fmsfe orphan-reaped rows 08:19). Disk 44% (189G/451G), watermark 85% — stale claim. Operator note: deployed binary (built 07:44) predates 7548196 (08:31) — restart on rebuilt binary before the digest re-files the anomaly. Triage: `docs/discovery/orphanclaim-disk99-triage-pf9c.md`. *Authored by a Hydra dogfood run.*
- task-breakdown: duplicate `TaskRoute`/`routeTaskPattern`/`TYPE_MARKER_ROUTES` declarations left in analyzer.mjs by the uqrw merge made the fixture suite fail with "Identifier 'TaskRoute' has already been declared" — older table-driven copy removed, superset implementation kept; all three suites green again. *Authored by a Hydra dogfood run.*
- Fleet-digest orphan-claim counter no longer counts human-escalated beads: beads whose verified-close was rejected 3/3 carry the `escalated` label and stay in_progress by design (no worker until a human or a Pan clear-escalation acts), so the digest re-fired the "P0 campaign anomaly: N in_progress bead(s) with no live registry worker" for exactly the beads the escalation gate already owns (hydra 2026-10-02 07:5x: bxzr/1h4v/wiu6/cxpf, tdd-validation-failed escalations; their workers had completed normally). Escalated beads are excluded from OrphanClaims; escalation_ttl (`overseer.escalation_ttl`) owns the aging. Tests: `TestCollectHealthDigestEscalatedBeadNotOrphan`. Triage: `docs/discovery/orphanclaim-escalated-triage-6d01.md`. *Authored by a Hydra dogfood run.*
- Pan health-digest orphan-claim counter now applies Charon's 5-minute claim grace: a bead claimed within the last 5m has no registry worker yet (spawn → status update → registry upsert is not atomic), so the digest's `orphanclaims` counter must not count it as orphaned. Without the grace the counter fired during every spawn wave and created a self-perpetuating filing loop — each campaign-anomaly bead Pan filed became itself a fresh in_progress claim with no worker yet, re-firing the anomaly (hydra 2026-10-02 02:34–02:48: hydra-ctix → hydra-qmf7 → hydra-t2e1 → hydra-yz8z filed in 13 min, every one a false alarm; on recheck all in_progress hydra beads had live registry workers with fresh heartbeats, and real disk usage was 43%, not the claimed 99%). Verified false-alarm triage + grace fix: `docs/discovery/orphanclaim-digest-grace-qmf7.md`. Tests: `TestCollectHealthDigestOrphanClaims_FreshClaimGrace`. *Authored by a Hydra dogfood run.*
- Fleet-digest orphan-claim false positive (P0 anomaly persistence loop, 2026-10-02 hydra-l867): the digest attributed live workers from `ListWorkers(working, parked)` only, so a bead whose worker row had been prematurely marked `completed` — while the worker process was still running and heartbeating every 30s (l867: row completed 02:52:38, PID alive until 03:1x) — counted as `in_progress with no live registry worker`, Pan re-filed a twin P0 report bead every judgment round. The digest now lists all worker states and treats a heartbeat-fresh row (<5m, mirroring Charon's heartbeatHungAfter) as live regardless of state; stale completed rows remain excluded. Tests: `TestCollectHealthDigestFreshHeartbeatCompletedRowNotOrphan`. *Authored by a Hydra dogfood run.*

Also: `ClassifyPushFailure` gains `CREDENTIAL` class — invalid/expired-token auth failures ("Invalid username or token", srv-telematics: 903 consecutive TRANSIENT-classified retries on a stale PAT) are no longer binned as TRANSIENT, so events distinguish "rotate the credential, human must fix" from "retry after backoff". Without the grace the counter fired during every spawn wave and created a self-perpetuating filing loop — each campaign-anomaly bead Pan filed became itself a fresh in_progress claim with no worker yet, re-firing the anomaly (hydra 2026-10-02 02:34–02:48: hydra-ctix → hydra-qmf7 → hydra-t2e1 → hydra-yz8z filed in 13 min, every one a false alarm; on recheck all in_progress hydra beads had live registry workers with fresh heartbeats, and real disk usage was 43%, not the claimed 99%). - Verified publish target fix for hydra-fdd9: haulmatic-devteams/hydra exists (API 200, push:true for daemon token); dry-run pushes of dogfood/hydra-fdd9 and dogfood/publish-hydra accepted. Root cause (org-rename flap + token scope) already remediated; triage in docs/discovery/publish-target-repo-not-found-fdd9.md. *Authored by a Hydra dogfood run.*
- Fail-closed spawn preflight (P0, bead fmsfe-a2u spawned 4x post-restart 2026-09-30): post-restart reconstructed beads can carry an empty `Type`/`Project`, silently no-opping every scheduler approval/epic/hold filter — the approval-gate bead executed as work, its worker exit was treated as completion, and the DAG released dependents while the `br` store still showed the gate open. Now every spawn path funnels through `Overseer.spawnWorker`, which (1) re-reads `Type`/`Project` from the bead store immediately before spawning and (2) fails closed (typed `spawn_blocked_type_missing` / `spawn_blocked_human_gate` events, no retry burned, auto-recheck next tick) when the effective Type is empty or a non-executable human-gate/container type. Also adds the spawn-funnel publish-identity preflight: a non-daemon-owned bead whose owner PAT is unresolved blocks the spawn with `spawn_blocked_identity_missing` and auto-resumes when the PAT lands (transient resolver errors fail open). *Authored by a Hydra dogfood run.*
- Spawn-funnel publish-identity preflight: before a worker is spawned (both DAG and retry-queue paths), `spawnPreflightPublishIdentity` resolves the bead owner's publish identity and, when the owner PAT is unresolved (`ErrPublishIdentityMissing`), refuses the spawn with a typed `spawn_blocked_identity_missing` event and re-checks next tick (auto-resume when the PAT lands). Moves the fail-closed owner-PAT check from close-time publish — where unresolved identities churned integration retries per bead (the fms-frontend 5gq/pmgh pathology) — to spawn time so doomed workers never burn provider tokens. Fail-open on absent resolver (unit tests/unconfigured daemons) and on non-identity resolver errors. `TestMultiUser_EndToEnd` now provisions alice's owner PAT. Tests: `TestSpawnPreflightPublishIdentity`. *Authored by a Hydra dogfood run.*
- Fixed: epic auto-close false positive (fmsfe incident, 2026-09-30 — epics closed with 31 open children). `CloseReadyEpics` judged from the in-memory DAG only; children invisible to the DAG build (their repo's `ListBeads` failed and was skipped, or otherwise filtered) left the epic with a vacuously-empty child set. `persistClosedEpics` now scans every project's bead stores for non-terminal dependents of the epic before writing the close: any open dependent or any listing error refuses the close (fail-closed, typed `epic_close_refused` event, 10m re-check throttle). Unresolvable project paths remain a refusal. Tests: `internal/core/epic_close_guard_test.go`.
- Publisher push-failure classification gains `NOT_FOUND` (new `PublishPathClass`): "Repository not found" / "does not appear to be a git repository" push errors are no longer binned as generic UNKNOWN — they route to the same PARK_LOCAL_LOUD action but carry their own class in `publish_path_classified` events, so the fleet can tell "remote renamed/moved or credential lost repo access — human must fix" apart from "transient, retry after backoff". Motivated by the 2026-10-01 hydra publish wedge: 2,371 not-found push failures over 8h while the repo flapped between `haulmatic-dev/hydra` and `haulmatic-devteams/hydra` during a transfer/rename. *Authored by a Hydra dogfood run.*

Motivated by the 2026-10-01 hydra publish wedge: 2,371 not-found push failures over 8h while the repo flapped between `haulmatic-dev/hydra` and `haulmatic-devteams/hydra` during a transfer/rename. - Publisher pr-merge failures now classified: the PR-route publish path (`PublishRepo` prMerge=true) previously emitted only a generic `publish_push_rejected reason=pr_merge_failed`; it now runs the same `ClassifyPushFailure` classifier as direct push and emits a `publish_path_classified` event. `NOT_FOUND` failures (org rename / owner PAT without visibility — hydra-m372: 91 not-found pushes in one day via a pre-rename buddhi PAT that 404s on haulmatic-devteams/hydra) additionally surface a loud credential-class message in the event, activity log, and returned reason: "retrying cannot succeed — human must fix the remote URL or the publish credential". - Daemon GitHub credential single-sourcing + preflight (2026-10-01 drift incident, 7h fleet grounding): `output.github_token_owner_user` makes the daemon service account resolve its credential from that user's `~/.hydra/.github-token` — the same resolution as the publish identity owner-PAT rule — and skips GH_TOKEN and the private `~/.git-credentials` copy entirely (fail-closed). New start-time credential preflight probes each project repo's origin with `git ls-remote` under the resolved publish identity (repo-level probe, so a token that is still valid but has LOST repo access — GitHub 404 — is caught, not just 401 auth failures); a stale result emits a typed `credential_stale{project}` event and auto-parks the project instead of silent publish failures. *Authored by a Hydra dogfood run.*
- Mandatory cross-verification doctrine for fix/bug/feature beads (thok-class false closure, 2026-10-01 audit): fix/bug `phaseGuidance` now leads with PRE-IMPLEMENTATION CROSS-VERIFICATION (reproduce the defect on a clean checkout of origin/<base>, verify-first close if not reproducible, grep the tree so duplicated machinery is never added) and every fix/bug/feature bead prompt ends with PRE-CLOSE CROSS-VERIFICATION (re-run the original reproduction, inspect `git diff origin/<base>...HEAD` so every verdict claim is literally in the diff, map each acceptance criterion to an executed check). Mechanical backstop: the verified-close gate emits a typed `empty_diff_completed` event when a done verdict is rejected over an empty or target-missing diff, making thok-class false closures observable in the event stream. Live-fire verified: a seeded already-fixed bug bead was verify-first closed with zero code changes. *Authored by a Hydra dogfood run.*
- Commit-step guard against embedded git repos (fmsfe-b8h wedge): agents occasionally scaffolded a nested repo (`git init` inside a worktree subdir), which made the close-time `git add -A` die with exit 128 "does not have a commit checked out" AFTER the full worker turn succeeded — every retry then burned a complete turn. `WorktreeManager.Commit/CommitBead` now scans the worktree (skipping `.beads`/node_modules/vendor/etc.) before staging, strips the embedded repo's `.git` metadata (files survive as plain content and are committed), and emits a typed `embedded_repo_detected{bead, path}` event so Pan sees the pattern. Workers also get a REPO HYGIENE prompt line banning `git init`/`git clone`/`go mod init` inside a workspace subdir. *Authored by a Hydra dogfood run.*
- Orphaned in_progress claim reconciliation (fms campaign wedge 2026-10-01): when a worker claims a bead and exhausts retries (or dies) without ever closing it, the bead sits `in_progress` — invisible to the scheduler (not ready) AND blocking all dependents (not closed), wedging the whole DAG ladder. The scheduler now reconciles such claims when the retry budget is exhausted and no live registry worker exists: the bead is reset to `open`, its retry entry is cleared (one fair retry), and a typed `orphan_claim_reset{bead, last_worker, attempts}` event is emitted; hard-parked labels (escalated/worktree-unavailable/integration-exhausted) are never reset. Pan's health digest also surfaces `orphanclaims=N` plus a P0 campaign-anomaly line for in_progress-with-no-live-worker, same class as the fleet-stall rule. *Authored by a Hydra dogfood run.*

- Fleet-automation prune sweep: Pan now runs a deterministic, zero-token `PrunableWedgeSweep` each shepherd tick before LLM judgment — when >=2 BLOCKED beads share a worktree/registration/spawn wedge reason, ONE project-scoped `repair:prune-worktree-registration` (reusing the existing live-worker, rate-limit, and repair-efficacy gates via the extracted `runRepair`) clears the whole cohort in batch. Successes and residual divergences are logged explicitly (`[PruneSweep]` lines); below-threshold cohorts and refused/failing repairs are reported, never swallowed. Prevents the "retry exhausted after 4 attempts" shared-wedge mode that previously required manual prunes (2026-09-21 srv-telematics, PROJECT-WEDGE). *Authored by Hydra bead fmsfe-a9p.*

- Integration base resolution now trusts the remote, not a possibly stale local symref: `ResolveIntegrationBase` asks `git ls-remote --symref origin HEAD` first (read-only, fail-open) before falling back to `refs/remotes/origin/HEAD` and the development/main candidates. Fixes the fms incident where a clone's stale `origin/HEAD -> main` redirected PRs at the dead `main` branch after the GitHub default moved to `development`; the remote-symref fallback also covers hung/offline remotes, and a successful ls-remote opportunistically reconciles a stale local `refs/remotes/origin/HEAD` via `git remote set-head` (best-effort, logged). *Authored by a Hydra dogfood run.*
- Integration-exhausted triage for **hydra-gh7j / hydra-37d9**: root cause of the retry exhaustion is NOT retry capacity — the `haulmatic-dev/hydra` GitHub remote went unreachable (`git push`/`fetch` exit 128 "remote: Repository not found.") between 22:24 UTC 2026-09-30 and 00:46 UTC 2026-10-01 and stays 404 via API under the daemon PAT; 44 identical failures across 9 beads (fmsfe-d1b/q70/53g/d68/hd7/4co/49s, hydra-gh7j, hydra-y1g5). The gh7j stale-`origin/HEAD` fix is complete on disk (`ace73e5`+`75e4f3a` on `hydra/wt-hydra-gh7j`), merges cleanly (merge-tree rc=0), unlanded and un-superseded; gates all green. Disposition: hold the durable park while the remote is 404, do NOT raise retry ceilings (deterministic failure — more attempts burn tokens like the 18.5M-token fms campaign), human-only action = restore/relocate the hydra repo then unpark; on return, cherry-pick the two feature commits (branch carries stale-integration deletions). Findings: `docs/discovery/integration-wedge-triage-37d9.md`. *Authored by a Hydra dogfood run.*
- Multi-repo discovered projects: IntegrationRetest, Publish, and DAG epic persistence no longer run against the multi-project parent directory (which has no `.beads` store and no git repo) — IntegrationRetest lists and retests parked beads per repo dir (`resolveRepoPath`), the publish loop publishes each repo individually with aggregated push-pending state, and epic auto-close persistence resolves the repo whose bead store contains the exact ID (also handling bead IDs whose store prefix, e.g. `fmsfe-`, matches no project name). Fixes the nipuna.j fms onboarding failures (`br list failed exit status 2`, `git rev-parse fatal: not a git repository`, `Could not find project path for epic`). *Authored by a Hydra dogfood run.*
- Fix close-not-sticking loop (srv-telematics u8z7/kher/qj74/5gq): `br close` that was **skipped** (open children) and exited NONZERO returned nil from `CloseBead` as "idempotent success" — Close() completed, the worker was marked completed, and the bead stayed open, respawning forever with no error anywhere. Two layers: (1) `execInDir`/`execInDirOnce` discarded stdout on CLI error, so skip/"already closed" wording never reached the classifier and only the ambiguous exit-3 shortcut fired; (2) the exit-3/nonzero-skip path never retried with `--force` and never verified terminality. Now: stdout is preserved in `CLIError` on error, explicit "already closed" wording is the only idempotent success, exit-3/worded skips force-close and the close is verified terminal — a still-skipped force close fails loud. *Authored by a Hydra dogfood run.*
- Shared-wedge triage for **hydra-28yq** (hydra-mhk2, hydra-xxq5, hydra-5ob8 simultaneous `integration-exhausted` hard-parks at 02:03–02:15 UTC 2026-09-30): not a systemic pipeline wedge — all three branches carried near-identical copies of the already-landed `isPRAlreadyExists` dedupe/existing-PR reuse fix (landed as e0c70a6/ecc6cb5/PR #418, precheck e0a3bce/PR #443) and conflicted with newer landed code, so re-running them together is contraindicated; mhk2 already resolved (labels cleared, PR #443), xxq5/5ob8 closed as superseded. Findings: `docs/discovery/integration-wedge-triage-28yq.md`. *Authored by a Hydra dogfood run.*
- Verify-first closure of fix bead **hydra-ntwy** (retry of hydra-mhk2's failed close): the mhk2 strand's substantive content is already integrated upstream — publisher pre-rebase conflict precheck landed via PR #443 (e0a3bce), rsyw docs conflict files landed newer (29ad719); a rebase experiment dropped the feature commit as already-upstream, so the wedge has no remaining unpublished work. Triage: `docs/discovery/integration-wedge-triage-ntwy.md`. *Authored by a Hydra dogfood run.*
- Shared-wedge triage for **hydra-xg6s** (hydra-mhk2, hydra-xxq5, hydra-5ob8): no capacity problem — mhk2 is the only open `integration-exhausted` strand and is blocked by 2 docs add/add conflicts (human rebase path already landed as `docs/discovery/integration-strand-triage-mhk2.md`, PR #438); xxq5/5ob8 are meta beads whose causes (PR-already-exists, WAL poison, stale bases) all landed upstream. No code change, no park clears; findings: `docs/discovery/integration-wedge-triage-xg6s.md`. *Authored by a Hydra dogfood run.*
- Adaptive integration-gate cooldown (integration-exhaustion storm hydra-mhk2/xxq5/5ob8 — three hard-parks within six minutes): consecutive gated integration failures on the same repo now double the next sibling's cooldown (30s→1m→2m→4m→8m, capped at `integrationBackoffMax`; any success resets to the base 30s), so a fleet-level burst of exhausting beads is staggered increasingly far apart instead of each re-racing the base branch after the same fixed window and burning an exhaustion-cap attempt on the shared wedge. In-gate success also clears the failure streak. Tests: `TestIntegrationGate_EscalatingCooldown`, `TestIntegrationGate_SuccessResetsStreak`. *Authored by a Hydra dogfood run.*
- Dedup closed **hydra-ut4z** ("Duplicate of integration-exhaustion storm filed for hydra-mhk2/dedupe-skipped-safe") as duplicate of pan-filed **hydra-mhk2**. Its fleet-concurrency proposal is already adjudicated: per-repo `integration_gate` serialization landed and `integration-wedge-triage-xg6s` (PR #439) verified no capacity problem (mhk2 blocked only by 2 docs add/add conflicts, human-only rebase). Findings: `docs/discovery/integration-wedge-triage-ut4z.md`. *Authored by a Hydra dogfood run.*
- Integration-exhausted park sweeper: new `overseer.integration_exhausted_sweep` knob (default 48h, 0 disables) — a periodic `IntegrationExhaustedSweeper` (1h cadence + at startup) flags every non-terminal bead carrying the durable `integration-exhausted` label older than the threshold with `needs-human` plus a typed `integration_exhausted_stale` event, so abandoned integration attempts surface for manual intervention or Pan retry-quarantine instead of staying hidden forever. The hard-park itself is never cleared (durable by design); in_progress and already-flagged beads are skipped. *Authored by a Hydra dogfood run.*

- Post-close verification sweep (GREEN for the pinned `postclose_sweep` contract): `PostCloseSweeper.scan` now re-runs each bead closed within 24h carrying `metadata["test_command"]` — first failure triggers one re-run; a passing re-run labels `postclose-flake` (no reopen), two failures reopen the bead with `postclose-reopened` plus a comment carrying BOTH failing-run outputs, and a third pass on an already-reopened bead adds `needs-human` instead of reopening again (bounded close/open loop). Opt-in via new `overseer.postclose_sweep` knob (default 0/disabled); typed `postclose_swept` events. *Authored by a Hydra dogfood run.*
- Publisher PR-first close made fully idempotent: `resolveOrCreatePR` now runs `gh pr list --head` BEFORE `gh pr create` and reuses the existing open PR (skipping create) when one tracks the dogfood head; a list error fails open to the create path, and the existing "already exists" error fallback still covers the list/create race. Prevents the integration-exhausted churn seen on srv-telematics (beads cycling integration-failed while their PRs sat open). *Authored by a Hydra dogfood run.*
- Tier-policy PR merge (operator publish policy 2026-09-29, tightened): new `output.pr_auto_merge_repos` allowlist (with `deploy.repo_path` implicitly included) selects which repos admin-merge their integration PRs (hydra tier-1) — and only when the PR base is `development` (conservative until FR-030 lands). Repos not on the list (product repos) NEVER auto-merge: the PR is created/left open and the close completes as waiting-on-human-merge via a new `PRWaitingError` sentinel — NOT an integration failure. This stops the `--admin` bypass of branch protection on product repos (merged_by=buddhiDsilva on srv-telematics PRs 377-389 defeated the four-eyes rule). Gate is unset-safe (legacy always-merge for tests/unconfigured daemons) and wired at daemon start. *Authored by a Hydra dogfood run.*

- Owner-identity publish (OPERATOR RULE 2026-09-29, fail-closed): every publish-path git/GitHub op — commit authorship, push, PR create/merge — now runs under the **bead owner's** PAT + git identity. New `internal/core/publish_identity.go`: bead → project → owning user → `/home/<user>/.hydra/.github-token`; unresolved owner PAT blocks the publish (typed `publish_identity_missing` event, no `GH_TOKEN`/daemon-credentials fallback for user-owned projects; the hydra service account's own credential remains valid for hydra-repo beads). `autoPRResolveToken` is owner-aware; the silent daemon-token fallback in `PublishViaPR`/`publishBranchViaPRCreate`/`maybeOpenAutoPR` is replaced by fail-closed identity resolution; worker commits carry `GIT_AUTHOR_*`/`GIT_COMMITTER_*` env and worktrees pin `git config user.name/user.email` from the owner's `~/.hydra/config.yaml`; integration-retest landing also fails closed. Operators MUST provision `/home/<user>/.hydra/.github-token` per user (host-setup §11). Tests: `internal/core/publish_identity_test.go`.
- Fast-path absorption for integration-parked beads (integration-exhausted spike 2026-09-29: five beads hard-parked within minutes, then converged back only one-per-hour through the retest stage's 1h retry interval): when a gated integration on a repo succeeds (per-repo integration gate success path), the integration-retest stage is notified — it opens a 5-minute fast-path window for that repo and runs an immediate background sweep, during which the per-bead `integration_retest.retry_interval` is bypassed (the `max_attempts` cap still applies), so a backlog of parked siblings is absorbed as soon as the integration path opens instead of one per hour. `SetIntegrationOpenNotifier` + `IntegrationRetestStage.NotifyRepoOpen`; fail-open, serialized per repo. Tests: `TestAllowAttemptFastPathBypassesRetryInterval`, `TestSetIntegrationOpenNotifier`, `TestNotifyRepoOpenSweepsParkedBeads`.
- Integration-exhausted triage for **hydra-isv5**: the bead's publish wedge (failed PR rebase, add/add conflicts in the two rsyw docs files) is already resolved — PR #422 merged (b421992 on origin/development); the quarantined branch's only unique content is superseded rsyw docs, so the close stands (abandon-with-summary) and the quarantine branch is droppable. The collision-prevention remedies proposed in the bead are already landed upstream (per-repo `integrationGate`, `publishWithRebaseRetry`). Findings: `docs/discovery/integration-wedge-triage-isv5.md`. *Authored by a Hydra dogfood run.*
- Shared-wedge triage for **hydra-fhhy** (named wedges hydra-isv5, hydra-5ob8): both causes resolved — isv5's doc-only rebase conflict landed via PR #422 (commit 882b0b7), and 5ob8's PR-already-exists proposal is already implemented (`resolveOrCreatePR` PR #418 + post-merge state-read retry). Live check: 0 `integration-exhausted` beads in the hydra and srv-telematics stores, 0 exhaust events on 2026-09-30. No code change, no park clears. Findings: `docs/discovery/integration-wedge-triage-fhhy.md`. *Authored by a Hydra dogfood run.*
- Fixed: pan file-bead dedupe is now persistent, not session-only. `filePanBead` refuses to create a bead when an open pan-filed bead with the same normalized title already exists (returns `pan.ErrFileBeadDuplicate`); `handleFileBead` treats that as a benign skip. Previously the signature map lived in daemon memory, so a restart re-filed the same finding — observed as pan-filed bead `hydra-jcj6` ("(dedupe skip)") filed for a wedge already tracked by `hydra-mhk2`. Tests: `TestFilePanBead_DuplicateOpenTitleSkipped`, `TestFileBeadDuplicateFilerResultSkips`.

Notable defect: the Pan file-bead pipeline filed a bead with placeholder title and lost the proposal payload — filing should refuse empty titles. Handoff: `docs/hydra/handoff/task-jcj6.md`. *Authored by a Hydra dogfood run.*
- Integration strand triage for **hydra-mhk2** (pan-filed publisher-wedge bead): strand is NOT superseded and NOT badly diverged — the branch carries unlanded work (`precheckRebaseConflicts` merge-tree pre-rebase gate in `internal/core/publisher.go`, `scripts/cache-cap-guard.sh`) and `git merge-tree` shows exactly TWO add/add conflicts, both docs-only (`integration-wedge-triage-rsyw.md`, `task-rsyw.md`); everything else auto-merges. - Integration-exhausted shared-wedge triage (hydra-rsyw/hydra-xxq5): the two named fleet causes are fixed — PR #389 was merged and the PR-already-exists publisher fix landed (PR #418, 1,056 `already exists` failures 06:00–17:00 UTC drop to 4 after), and host disk is at 78% (below the 85% watermark). - Post-merge PR state verification now retries through GitHub API eventual consistency instead of counting a stale read as an integration failure (shared wedge, 2026-09-29 18:22–18:36 UTC: hydra-uqxe PR #401 merged 18:27:59Z but the 18:28:02Z state read returned OPEN; srv-telematics burr PR #380 merged 18:22:29Z yet re-parked minutes later). `defaultAutoPRGhMerge` now retries the `gh pr view` state read up to 4 times with 2s×n backoff before failing, so a just-merged PR settles to MERGED instead of driving the bead to `integration-exhausted` hard-park. Both beads' work is confirmed landed (PRs MERGED) — their parks were stale. Test: `TestAutoPRGhMerge_MergedExitZeroStaleOpenState`.
- Shared-wedge triage for the integration-exhausted mass-parks reported by **hydra-xxq5** and **ajantha-srv-telematics-burr**: the clustering was a poisoned br WAL index ("database is busy (recovery in progress)") — recovered via `br doctor migrate-schema recover` ×2 — not integration-capacity exhaustion; retry backoff already exists (integration_retest, per-repo integration gate) and superseded-branch parks stay human-only. Findings: `docs/discovery/integration-wedge-triage-2vuc.md`. *Authored by a Hydra dogfood run.*
- Fix close-not-sticking loop (srv-telematics u8z7/kher/qj74/5gq): `br close` that was **skipped** (open children) and exited NONZERO returned nil from `CloseBead` as "idempotent success" — Close() completed, the worker was marked completed, and the bead stayed open, respawning forever with no error anywhere. Two layers: (1) `execInDir`/`execInDirOnce` discarded stdout on CLI error, so skip/"already closed" wording never reached the classifier and only the ambiguous exit-3 shortcut fired; (2) the exit-3/nonzero-skip path never retried with `--force` and never verified terminality. Now: stdout is preserved in `CLIError` on error, explicit "already closed" wording is the only idempotent success, exit-3/worded skips force-close and the close is verified terminal — a still-skipped force close fails loud. *Authored by a Hydra dogfood run.*
- Publisher rebase precheck (hydra-mhk2): before FR-011 rebase-retry touches a checkout or branch, `git merge-tree --write-tree --name-only` previews the replay against `origin/<base>`; a predicted conflict (e.g. add/add on handoff/discovery docs) now fails closed with the file list and a `publish_rebase_precheck_conflict` event BEFORE any rebase starts — previously the rebase ran and left the daemon's own checkout in an in-progress conflicted state, wedging all later publishing. Precheck execution errors fail open (log and proceed). *Authored by a Hydra dogfood run.*
- Publisher wedge triage (hydra-mhk2): the /opt/dev-tools/components/hydra publish failure (add/add conflicts in docs/discovery/integration-wedge-triage-rsyw.md and docs/hydra/handoff/task-rsyw.md, commit a15231c) left the main checkout with a cherry-pick in progress and diverged 2/58 from origin/development. Conflict resolution is human-only by design; the code remedy (pre-rebase conflict detection) is implemented in this change. Findings: `docs/discovery/publisher-rebase-precheck-mhk2.md`.

### Documentation
- Roadmap: CASS knowledge federation (`docs/roadmap/cass-knowledge-federation-20260928.md`): CASS playbooks are local per user — root's ~2,552 curated rules are invisible to the fleet (the daemon injects from hydra's 13-rule playbook). Proposes one-way curated sync (score threshold + category allowlist + sensitive-pattern denylist) into the hydra store with a human-reviewed reverse lane for worker-discovered rules; explicitly rejects a shared symlink (write races, relevance bleed, feedback mixing) and hindsight-mirroring (drift).
### Documentation
- PRD: Autonomous Resolution Layer (`docs/PRDs/autonomous-resolution-layer-20260928.md`): converts the 2026-09-28 autonomy analysis (570 integrate failures from 2 beads, 86 quarantine branches, fleet idle 5h on a mechanical conflict, daemon hindsight injection dead at 0% for weeks) into four fix sets — spawn-side conflict prevention (per-bead handoff fragments, shared-infra exclusivity lock, scope serialization), PR-first close with capped auto rebase beads, Pan as bead-filing diagnostician (P1, signature-deduped, capped) with failure-signature clustering and a quarantine→Pan candidate connection, tiered hydra self-repo auto-merge/canary-deploy behind a fail-closed classifier, plus fail-open degradation counters and an operator park/quarantine API.

Stale `escalated`/`needs-human`/`verified-close-escalated` labels removed for one fair scheduler retry; the daemon-side TTL scanner + Pan `clear-escalation` verb (already landed) prevent recurrence. ### Fixed
- Duplicate-PR race no longer hard-parks beads `integration-exhausted` (fleet wedge 2026-09-29, 14:56–15:03 UTC: hydra-242b/ycmu/9ydi + telematics qj74/otae/0po4/f10l/dkqc/38ot/04jq/burr): when `gh pr create` fails with "a pull request for branch ... already exists", the publisher now reuses the existing PR — `isPRAlreadyExists` detection in `PublishViaPR` and `publishBranchViaPROnce` falls through to `gh pr merge` on the existing PR, and the PR-first close treats the reuse as landed instead of counting a consecutive integration failure. Previously the open-but-unmerged PR (the actual landing vehicle) triggered `consecutive_failures_capped` hard-park on every retry. Tests: `TestIsPRAlreadyExists_Classification`, `TestPublishAfterIntegrate_PRAlreadyExists_Benign`.
- Park no longer blocks Charon orphan recovery (live incident 2026-09-29 10:20 UTC): recoverOrphanInProgressBeads skipped all orphan handling for parked projects, so their dead workers' `running` tracker rows were never reaped — the daemon tracked 6/6 phantom worker slots with 0 live processes and the whole fleet stalled ("All worker slots filled (6/6), skipping bead discovery") until restart. Park's contract is stop-NEW-spawns; the scheduler already consults ProjectParkInfo on every spawn path, so recovery (stale-row reap + orphan reopen) now runs for parked projects too. Test: `TestRecoverOrphanInProgressBeads_ParkedCampaignStillRecovers`.
- Hard-park triage for hydra-242b (2026-09-29): pan-filed escalation-wedge bead's branch (`escalation_expiry_hours`, 48h) is a superseded iteration — development already has the EscalationTTLScanner (`overseer.escalation_ttl`, 72h) plus the `clear-escalation` Pan repair; branch conflicts with development in `internal/core/config.go`. Disposition: close/abandon the bead, do NOT clear the park, do NOT rebase. Evidence: `docs/hydra/handoff/task-242b.md`.

- Integration triage for the hydra-d778 / hydra-q5ey / hydra-014y shared wedge (2026-09-29): the parks persisted because the LOCAL development checkout was 29 commits behind origin (stale base → integration merge failures), not a remote-path fault. ### Added
- Per-repo integration serialization + quarantine re-attempt verb (shared integration wedge, bead hydra-z362): the close path's integrate/PR step and the worktree reconciler's stranded-branch merge now run under a per-repo integration gate (`internal/core/integration_gate.go`) — at most one bead integrates against a repo at a time, and after a gated failure sibling beads wait a 30s repo cooldown before starting (success clears it), so parallel closes competing for the same base branch stop burning each other's integration-exhaustion caps (2026-09-29 telematics wedge: 4 beads exhausted within one campaign). New Pan repair `retry-quarantine` (`internal/core/quarantine_reattempt.go`): for a stranded-conflict bead whose `hydra/quarantine/<key>` branch now merge-trees clean against the (moved) base, restores `hydra/wt-<key>`, deletes the quarantine branch, removes the label, reopens the bead, and emits `quarantine_reattempt{result:requeued}`; still conflicted → refused, bead stays quarantined. Whitelisted in Pan's prompt with a rescue recipe. Tests: `integration_gate_test.go`, `quarantine_reattempt_test.go` (real git).
- Publish-path policy classifier + decision table (FR-010 follow-up, 2026-09-29): every failed git push is now classified by a pure function (`ClassifyPushFailure` in `internal/core/publish_path.go`) into PROTECTED (GH013/GH006/protected-branch/rule-violation/required-review), STALE (non-fast-forward, fetch-first — checked BEFORE protected markers since GitHub phrases divergence as "remote rejected (non-fast-forward)"), TRANSIENT (DNS/auth/timeout/RPC), or UNKNOWN, and routed by `ResolvePublishPath`: accepted+hydra-owned → done; PROTECTED → idempotent PR (existing `maybeOpenAutoPR` already lists open PRs by head before create, never force-pushes); STALE → rebase retry (existing FR-011 recovery, cap 2); TRANSIENT/UNKNOWN → local commit + loud typed event `publish_path_classified` + retry — no silent unpublished accumulation (the 16h wedge lesson). Ownership gate `IsHydraOwnedBranch`: only `hydra/`, `dogfood/`, or configured `output.writable_branch_prefixes` count as published destinations; a push that lands elsewhere is surfaced loudly. Tests: `internal/core/publish_path_test.go` (table-driven, no network).
- Semantic (embedding-based) fix-failure clustering (FR-020 follow-up, bead hydra-n5qt): after an exact FR-020 signature miss, the close path now embeds the extracted failure cause and compares it by cosine similarity (default threshold 0.82) against the recorded causes of open meta fix beads, parking near-duplicate root causes against the existing meta bead (event `fix_semantic_clustered`) instead of filing another per-bead fix bead. This closes the gap where string-signature matching split ONE root cause into 4 clusters during the 2026-09-29 telematics drain (4 overlapping fix beads, 10+ duplicate PRs on the same conflicts). Opt-in via `validation.semantic_clustering: {enabled, threshold, embed_base_url, embed_model, embed_api_key_env}` — inert without `enabled`/an API key; every embedding failure fails open to string clustering; embeddings cached in-process by signature hash (bounded at 1024 entries). Tests: `internal/core/semantic_cluster_test.go` incl. a replay of the telematics set (4 same-root-cause failures → 1 meta bead, 3 parked).
- Escalation TTL / aging (stale human escalations): `overseer.escalation_ttl` (default 72h, 0 = off) — a periodic scanner (1h cadence, once at startup) demotes non-terminal beads whose durable `escalated` label has aged past the TTL: label swapped for `escalation-expired`, typed event `escalation_expired`, bead returns to the ready queue. Escalations were previously never adjudicated, permanently wedging the scheduler slots they held (measured 91h–290h stale: hydra-wdp, hydra-cip, hydra-gvh, hydra-ol7). `in_progress` beads are skipped so a live worker finishing under the stay is never raced. Tests: `internal/core/escalation_ttl_test.go`.
- Pan `repair:clear-escalation` verb for stale human escalations: beads blocked on the `escalated` label for days (95–295h observed) with no human response now have a daemon-side hygiene repair — Pan may clear the `escalated`/`needs-human`/`verified-close-escalated` labels, reopen the bead, and reset its retry counter for one fair scheduler retry; the escalation label re-fires if the same failure recurs. Refuses when no escalation label is present. Handler in `internal/core/service_starter.go`; whitelist + judgment-prompt recipe in `internal/pan/pan.go`; tests updated in `internal/pan/pan_test.go`, `internal/pan/file_bead_test.go`.
- Semantic (embedding-based) fix-failure clustering (FR-020 follow-up, bead hydra-n5qt): after an exact FR-020 signature miss, the close path now embeds the extracted failure cause and compares it by cosine similarity (default threshold 0.82) against the recorded causes of open meta fix beads, parking near-duplicate root causes against the existing meta bead (event `fix_semantic_clustered`) instead of filing another per-bead fix bead. This closes the gap where string-signature matching split ONE root cause into 4 clusters during the 2026-09-29 telematics drain (4 overlapping fix beads, 10+ duplicate PRs on the same conflicts). Opt-in via `validation.semantic_clustering: {enabled, threshold, embed_base_url, embed_model, embed_api_key_env}` — inert without `enabled`/an API key; every embedding failure fails open to string clustering; embeddings cached in-process by signature hash (bounded at 1024 entries). Tests: `internal/core/semantic_cluster_test.go` incl. a replay of the telematics set (4 same-root-cause failures → 1 meta bead, 3 parked).
- Operator park/unpark/quarantine API (FR-033, autonomous-resolution-layer PRD): new endpoints under `/api/v1` replace direct sqlite manipulation of `registry.db` for operator actions. `POST /projects/:name/park` and `POST /projects/:name/unpark` (optional `{reason}`) write the project-park row the scheduler and Charon already consult, via the daemon as single writer; `GET /quarantine` lists `hydra/quarantine/*` branches and open beads carrying quarantine labels (`stranded-work`, `stranded-conflict`, `integration-unverified`, `integration-exhausted`, `worktree-unavailable`) per configured project; `POST /quarantine/:bead/clear` (`{project, labels?}`) removes only quarantine labels from a bead — the label set is intersected so arbitrary labels cannot be stripped. Every mutation emits a typed audit event on the shepherd stream (`project_parked`, `project_unparked`, `quarantine_cleared`, NFR-002); registry unavailable answers 503. Tests: `internal/api/operator_test.go`.
- Tier-1 canary deploy with automatic rollback (FR-031, autonomous-resolution-layer PRD): opt-in `deploy:` config block (`enabled`, `repo_path`, `binary_path`, `verify_script`, `build_timeout`, default off per NFR-004). After a successful publish on the configured repo, the orchestrator enforces the drain precondition (skips with a typed `deploy_skipped_workers_in_flight` event if any worker is active), pins max_workers to 0 for the build window, backs up the daemon binary, builds and atomically swaps in the new one, restarts, runs `scripts/verify-deploy.sh`, and rolls back to the previous binary + restart on any failure. Every step emits typed deploy events on the shepherd stream (NFR-002). Also ships the FR-030-lite path-based tier classifier (`ClassifyTier1` — any doubt → tier-1g) and `AutoMergeIfTier1` (squash-merge only when all touched paths classify tier-1 AND CI checks are green; fails closed toward human). Tests: `internal/core/deploy_test.go`.
- Auto rebase on PR conflict, cap 2 then human (FR-011, 2026-09-29): when a PR publish (`PublishViaPR` from the publisher tick, `PublishBranchViaPR` from the FR-010 PR-first close) fails with a merge-conflict error, the branch is rebased onto `origin/<base>` (working branch rebased in the checkout with `--autostash` preserving human WIP; worktree branches rebased in a detached scratch worktree, ref moved after, no checkout touched, hooks disabled for replayed commits) and the publish retried, up to `MaxPRRebaseAttempts` (2). A failed rebase or a still-conflicted PR after the cap fails closed with an explicit "human review needed" error and emits `publish_rebase_attempted` / `publish_rebase_exhausted` events; callers already treat publish errors as not-landed, so escalation surfaces via the existing quarantine/Activity channels. Tests: `internal/core/publisher_rebase_test.go` (conflict detection, retry cap, non-conflict passthrough, real-git scratch rebase).
- Fail-open degradation counters in the Pan health digest (FR-032, autonomous-resolution-layer PRD): every fail-open subsystem now records attempt/success counters (`internal/degrade`, process-global, `HYDRA_DEGRADE_WINDOW` override, default 24h tumbling window) and a subsystem at 0% success over the window (>= 3 attempts) renders a `host: degradations=[name 0/N]` line in every Pan judgment — same visibility class as the judge-degradation alert (PR #275). Instrumented: hindsight recall (`agents/context-processor.go` — the 500ms-timeout bug, bead hydra-bye7, would have fired this within one tick), worker retain (`cmd/worker/retain.go`), and Pan store scans (`internal/core/pan_health.go`). Worker-process counters are process-local; daemon-side subsystems surface in the digest directly.
